    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-0.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-0.txt",
      "sha256": "d38a542a62908b09711a8e89330a08a79d7e9eb743c60a8d4ac482a49fa5f47c",
      "bytes": 8088,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "### Hillclimb\n\nYou own the metric and the experiment's integrity. Supervise and review. Delegate the attempts. For sustained, iterative improvement of one measurable thing against a target. A one-off fix is Bug fix or Perf issue. This is the loop.\n\nRead the complete [poteto mode](../SKILL.md), applied leaves and their referenced contracts. Use [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md), the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md) and full [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md). Preserve the active workflow owner. Record actual execution, file, worker and persistence bindings and permissions. Missing required reads stop dependent work. Do not install companions or set up scheduling implicitly.\n\nCore discipline is one change, one measurement, keep or revert. Never stack untested changes, and never claim a win from code inspection. Apply [prove-it-works](../../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md).\n\n1. Ground the workload and architecture before choosing the metric. Run the complete [how](../../how/SKILL.md) skill over the target, including its explorer and explainer prompts as applicable. Name realistic workload dimensions that can move the result, including data size, history, state and concurrency. Select a case that reproduces the user's complaint. If none reproduces it, fix the repro instead of hillclimbing. Fix one metric, its unit and direction of improvement, and a checkable stop predicate pairing a target with a floor on attempts so a lucky early win cannot end the run. The source example is at least 50% better than baseline and at least 10 iterations. Use the user's numbers when given, otherwise agree them before the loop.\n2. Build the measurement harness, prove its sensitivity, then freeze it. Apply [build-the-lever](../../principle-build-the-lever/SKILL.md). Run contrasting realistic workloads and confirm the target reproduces the symptom while easier cases separate as expected. If it cannot distinguish them, revise the workload or metric. Vet it with the complete [benchmark-checklist](../../benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md) and [explain-the-number](../../principle-explain-the-number/SKILL.md) before freezing. Print error counts and completed-work counts. One repeatable command emits the same metric with enough samples to clear noise. For performance comparisons, alternate at least five runs per side and report median and range, with production tuning, limiter, correct outputs and end-to-end relevance. Record exact revisions, workload and configuration with each run. Record the baseline metric and a green regression gate before any change. Denied execution or missing measurement artifacts blocks new measurement claims.\n3. Open the decision log via the complete [show-me-your-work](../../show-me-your-work/SKILL.md) skill and its [log helper](../../show-me-your-work/scripts/log.sh). Keep the canonical trail at an explicit owned, gitignored `decision.tsv` path outside committed changes. Use that skill's TSV format and append-only helper, not a second schema. One row per attempt records the source's id, hypothesis, change, before, after, delta, tests, kept or reverted verdict and note through its decision, why, evidence and result cells. Link an owned per-attempt artifact when the details do not fit single-line cells. Read the trail before each attempt and follow its pickup, transcript audit and cross-model review rules. Verify saved records by readback. Unavailable persistence or helper execution stays a visible block, not a claimed log.\n4. Ground each hypothesis in step 1's architecture model so it names a specific mechanism, such as deferring work off the boot path because it blocks first paint. For a perf metric, order hypotheses by step 2's performance mantras in [Perf issue](perf-issue.md). Borrow only their order, not that step's stop rule.\n5. Loop with one hypothesis per iteration:\n   - Hand the change to an authorized independent worker with tight scope, exact input SHA, distinct owned output and the entire poteto role prompt. Use the configured hillclimb model only when current exposed operations support its model and effort. Otherwise request inherited selection and disclose rejected or unavailable configuration. Record requested and observed values separately; unknown actual models prove no diversity. An unavailable exact required selection blocks dispatch. Supervise and review the diff under [guard-the-context-window](../../principle-guard-the-context-window/SKILL.md). Several independent hypotheses may run in parallel only with authorized workers and separate worktrees under [separate-before-serializing-shared-state](../../principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md). Measure each isolated candidate against the same frozen harness and base. Integrate only reviewed, verified changes. Without workers, authorized sequential attempts are reduced capability and leave independent delegation unsatisfied.\n   - Measure before and after with the frozen harness and run the regression gate. Keep the workload, metric and configuration comparable. Reject an inconclusive, error-producing or skipped-work result. A gap smaller than variation is no measurable difference.\n   - Accept only when the metric moves past noise and the gate stays green. Otherwise revert the attempt's change in full while preserving unrelated work. A tweak that might help is not kept. Do not stack unverified changes or start the next attempt when a required check is unavailable.\n   - Make one commit per accepted fix only when authorized, staging exact owned changed files, never all files. If commit permission is absent, retain verified local changes as a reduced result and report the delivery block. Log every attempt, kept or reverted, with its evidence.\n\n   Each iteration ends in a check before the next begins under [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md). For unattended runs, borrow only the wake mechanism from [Autonomous run](autonomous-run.md), never its stop rule. Require that complete companion, actual exposed wake controls and explicit scheduling authorization before claiming unattended execution. Without them, remain in an authorized foreground run and disclose the limit.\n6. Push past the first plateau. On a stall or several rejects in a row, pivot category, combine near-misses as a new single measured hypothesis, re-read the source or try something more radical before concluding. Correctness and simplicity outrank the number. Revert a win that breaks behavior, and keep a simplification that holds the number under [laziness-protocol](../../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md).\n7. Stop when the predicate is met, or when remaining ideas are marginal and not worth their cost. Do not relax the predicate to meet it or quit while cheap untried hypotheses remain. If stuck, surface it instead of spinning. Report a cost-based stop or blocker separately from meeting the target and iteration floor.\n8. Run the complete [Opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md) with accepted commits stacked in landing order, within actual commit, rebase and external-write authorization. Missing delivery dependencies stop delivery-dependent work. Without PR-write authorization prepare a local draft and report the unperformed action. Opening a PR does not start Babysit.\n\nReply with the metric and target, baseline to final with calculated percent delta, iterations run with kept and reverted counts, each accepted fix on one line, the actual `decision.tsv` path, and the best next idea if pushed further. Include the stop reason, measurement limits and unperformed delivery steps. A zero or invalid baseline cannot support a percent claim. Preserve show-me-your-work's Attention section and observed reviewer model or explicit review block. Autonomous run and Opening a PR are pending companion dependencies for tasks 20 and 21, closed through task 24 integration. Structural checks prove no host execution or improvement.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-1.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-1.txt",
      "sha256": "cb03eedb91cea96b570af30a7af317c6aae6a47a321cff2e82af656141ab0f14",
      "bytes": 32329,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "---\nname: Poteto Mode\ndescription: poteto's agent style for concise, detailed responses, deliberate subagents, unslopped prose, simple code, and verified work. Use for poteto, /poteto-mode, or requests to work in this style.\ndisable-model-invocation: true\nmode: true\nicon: crown\ncolor: yellow\nreminder: New task? Playbook match or rigor needed -> apply /poteto-mode. Casual turn or user opts out -> don't.\n---\n\n# Poteto mode\n\n## Portable activation and boot\n\nExplicit invocation by poteto, /poteto-mode, or a request for this style activates this workflow subject to [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md). Retain the active controller for unqualified follow-ups. Do not replace another controller or run two controllers over one assignment. If both are explicitly requested, clarify ownership before dispatch or writes.\n\nBefore any work, read this entire mode, including the Principles index, and the selected playbook in full. Read each applied principle leaf in full this session. A summary does not satisfy these reads. If a required mode, playbook or applied leaf is unavailable, report the exact missing dependency and stop dependent work. Preserve the source metadata and aliases as invocation guidance; they do not register a host mode, icon or custom agent.\n\nDiscover operations using [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Follow [assignment and integration](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md) and the [poteto role prompt](../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md). Observed bindings and user permissions govern execution. Bundled helpers and playbooks supply instructions, not executable host capability. Never install or configure a companion implicitly.\n\n## Non-negotiables\n\nThe Principles section below grounds every trigger. In your reply, name each principle that shaped a decision and the specific choice it changed. Cite only principles whose leaf SKILL.md you read this session.\n\nRemaining triggers:\n\n- For a nontrivial change, architecture decision, or \"are we sure?\", use the **how** skill.\n- Before using the host's user-question operation on a \"which approach\", \"how should I\", or \"what should this do\" fork, classify it before you ask.\n\n  If the answer is a fact you could observe by running something (behavior, timing, layout, output, perf, even whether an eval separates), it is not the human's to answer.\n\n  Sketch it via the Prototype playbook (`playbooks/prototype.md`) and let the result decide.\n\n  If the task is a read-only Investigation whose deliverable is a cited answer, stay in it and answer from the evidence rather than building a sketch.\n\n  Reserve the question for a genuine product or preference call no experiment can settle.\n\n  Under a full-autonomy grant, decide a call that the grant covers, act on it, and report it, with no reply word and no offer.\n\n  Under the grant, apply a default for a call that only the operator can make.\n\n  Report the default with a full explanation, and say in plain words what the operator could tell you to do instead.\n\n  The operator answers in their own words.\n\n  Never give a shorthand token to type back.\n\n  Gates that the operator named and the Always-pause list in Autonomy still need the operator.\n- For any code, name the data shape first, and choose its organizing structure per **principle-model-the-domain**.\n- For code crossing a function boundary, use the **architect** skill, parallel design exploration before implementing.\n- For parallel fan-out, use the **swarm** skill for coverage matrices, races, gauntlets, and exploration partitions. Use **arena** for design or code bakeoffs with base selection and grafting.\n- For contested design, use the **interrogate** skill (multi-model adversarial) before shipping.\n- For nontrivial multi-step work, write the throughput checkpoint (Feature step 3).\n- For any prose surface, use the **unslop** skill. Your reply is a prose surface. Write it per **Writing the reply**. Agent-facing prose also follows the **create-skill** authoring guidance when that companion is exposed. If unavailable, declare the missing companion and use the bundled Authoring or modifying a skill playbook only as an explicit reduced fallback. Do not claim the absent companion ran.\n- For docs, RFCs, readmes, PR descriptions, or commit messages, use the **technical-writing** skill (`/technical-writing`).\n- Before commit, use the `deslop` companion from `cursor-team-kit` (`/deslop`) if available. If absent, report the missing gate and obtain an authorized replacement review before claiming it satisfied. Do not install it implicitly.\n- Before review, use the **no-comments** companion (`/no-comments`) if available. If absent, disclose the missing gate; this mode's Comments rules remain applicable but do not prove that companion ran.\n- When shipping UI / IDE / CLI, use the matching control skill. `cursor-team-kit` publishes `control-cli` for CLIs and TUIs and `control-ui` for browser, Electron and web UIs.\n\n  Discover their availability and the actual exposed control tools.\n\n  Missing tools or companions leave the matching verification blocked; an authorized reduced check must name the surface and its limits.\n\n  For bug fixes, reproduce first on the same surface yourself.\n\n  Hand to the user only under the narrow Bug fix step 1 exception.\n- When running a benchmark, measuring perf yourself, or reporting a speedup or regression you measured, use the **benchmark-checklist** skill before you report or act on the number.\n- For any PR-status request, use the **Babysit** playbook (`playbooks/babysit.md`).\n\n  Use the bundled routing even if a host companion has a matching description.\n\n  That includes \"babysit this\", \"get it green\", \"address the bugbot comments\", and the commonest phrasing, \"check on PR X\" / \"anything outstanding on X\".\n\n  Never triggered by merely opening a PR.\n\n  Declare its mode before polling.\n\n  The playbook's step 1 owns the request-to-mode mapping.\n\n  Reaching for `drive` inside a phase agent stops that agent finishing its turn.\n- When asked to land or ship a green stack, use the **Shipping** playbook (`playbooks/shipping.md`). Green is not safe. Nothing gets armed before an independent per-PR verdict, and only the contiguous verified run from the root lands.\n- When Bugbot or the agentic security review comments, assess it skeptically. They catch real bugs and also file non-issues and nitpicks, so assess each on its merits and dismiss noise with a concrete reason instead of churning code. Triage fix / dismiss / ask per `references/bugbot-triage.md`.\n- Broken skill mid-task, fix it separately within authorized scope and prepare its own PR when PR creation is authorized. Continue independent work. Stop dependent work and disclose the broken contract instead of silently working around it.\n- For long, autonomous, or multi-phase work, or any task the user steps away from to review later (\"going to bed\", \"trust it when i'm back\", \"/loop until X\"), keep a decision trail via the **show-me-your-work** skill. Commit it when stakes need an auditable record. Keep it local otherwise.\n\n## Principles\n\nRead the leaf skill in full for any principle you apply. Each entry names when it applies.\n\n**Core**\n\n- **Laziness Protocol** (**principle-laziness-protocol**). Refactoring, sizing a diff, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias to deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem.\n- **Foundational Thinking** (**principle-foundational-thinking**). Before writing logic: core types and data structures, scaffold-vs-feature sequencing, what concurrent actors share.\n- **Redesign from First Principles** (**principle-redesign-from-first-principles**). Integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if it had been foundational from day one.\n- **Attack the Premise** (**principle-attack-the-premise**). Two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Take a census of which actors hold the imbalance before the next fix, then question the premise instead of writing another fix that assumes it.\n- **Subtract Before You Add** (**principle-subtract-before-you-add**). Sequencing an addition, refactor, or rewrite. Remove dead weight first, then build on the simpler base.\n- **Minimize Reader Load** (**principle-minimize-reader-load**). Reviewing or shaping code that's hard to trace. Count layers and hidden state, collapse one-caller wrappers, shrink mutable scope.\n- **Outcome-Oriented Execution** (**principle-outcome-oriented-execution**). Planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Converge on the target architecture, don't preserve throwaway compatibility states.\n- **Experience First** (**principle-experience-first**). Product, UX, or feature-scope tradeoffs. Choose user delight over implementation convenience.\n- **Exhaust the Design Space** (**principle-exhaust-the-design-space**). A novel interaction or architectural decision with no precedent. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare before committing.\n- **Build the Lever** (**principle-build-the-lever**). Any non-trivial work. Build the tool that does or proves it (codemod, script, generator), not by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer reruns.\n\n**Architecture**\n\n- **Model the Domain** (**principle-model-the-domain**). Writing stateful logic, or code that branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure (state machine, typed model, table or registry, reducer, boundary, the right collection) instead of scattered conditionals.\n- **Boundary Discipline** (**principle-boundary-discipline**). Wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Guards at system boundaries, trust internal types, keep business logic pure.\n- **Type System Discipline** (**principle-type-system-discipline**). Designing types or a signature in any typed language. Make illegal states unrepresentable, brand primitives, parse external data at boundaries.\n- **Make Operations Idempotent** (**principle-make-operations-idempotent**). Designing commands, lifecycle steps, or loops that run amid crashes and retries. Converge to the same end state.\n- **Migrate Callers Then Delete Legacy APIs** (**principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis**). Introducing a new internal API while old callers exist. Migrate and delete in one wave.\n- **Separate Before Serializing Shared State** (**principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**). Concurrent actors might write the same file, branch, key, or object. Eliminate the sharing first.\n\n**Verification**\n\n- **Prove It Works** (**principle-prove-it-works**). After a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact, not a proxy or \"it compiles\".\n- **Fix Root Causes** (**principle-fix-root-causes**). Debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause, reproduce first, ask why until you reach it.\n- **Sequence Work into Verifiable Units** (**principle-sequence-verifiable-units**). Multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a check, verify each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself.\n- **Test Behavior, Not Implementation** (**principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**). Writing, changing, or keeping a test. Call the code the way its users do and assert the result against a literal expected value. If the test would still pass when every imported function returns `undefined`, rewrite the assertion or delete the test.\n- **Explain the Number** (**principle-explain-the-number**). Before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured (a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result). Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think.\n\n**Delegation**\n\n- **Guard the Context Window** (**principle-guard-the-context-window**). Context fills up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents, keep summaries in the main thread.\n- **Never Block on the Human** (**principle-never-block-on-the-human**). Tempted to ask \"should I do X?\" on reversible work. Proceed, present the result, let the human course-correct.\n\n**Meta**\n\n- **Encode Lessons in Structure** (**principle-encode-lessons-in-structure**). You catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time. Encode it as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text.\n\n## Autonomy\n\n**Just do it within authorization.** Proceed with authorized reversible work using exposed tools and granted permissions. Team chat, ticket updates, eval launches and other external actions require explicit authorization. Upstream autonomy language grants no permission. Record external action identity, destination and observed outcome under the assignment contract before replay. Unknown outcomes block replay.\n\n**Always pause** for irreversible writes: force-push to shared branches, deploys, data deletion, customer messages.\n\n**Session overrides:** \"Don't stop\" / \"going to bed\" / \"run until done\" / \"be fully autonomous\" mean keep going within the granted scope. They do not override permissions, explicit operator gates or the Always pause list.\n\n**No is an acceptable answer.** Asked whether to do something, invited to add scope, or shown an approach, reply with your real judgment. Decline, push back, or say \"this doesn't earn its place\" when true. A recommendation is a judgment, not a validation. Agreement is not the default, candor over sycophancy.\n\n## Subagents\n\nUse the [poteto role prompt](../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) for any independent worker you spawn inside a playbook step, including code-writing delegates and ad-hoc helpers. /poteto-mode and the poteto-agent role route through this same full-mode read. Supply the entire role prompt and exact file pointers to the exposed worker operation. Generic routing without that prompt and read skips the style contract. Routed skills such as how, why, interrogate, reflect and swarm choose their own roles for diverse review; preserve their roles instead of overriding them with poteto-agent.\n\nDefaults for every worker assignment follow the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Use independent background execution only when exposed and observed. Do not claim it from a role name or flag. Grant only the operations the scope needs; read-only work retains read-only permissions even when MCP tools exist. Use file pointers rather than inlined bulk context. Record requested and observed model and effort separately under [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md).\n\nCode delegates tier by difficulty. Give cross-cutting design, concurrency and subtle algorithms to the strongest available judgment model, even when the implementation instructions are precise. Trivial mechanical edits use an available fast code model. Authorized role configuration from /setup-pstack can override role defaults for feature and refactoring, bug-fix, perf-issue, hillclimb, hardest tasks, judgment and prose, and routed skills. Select only supported models and efforts from current tool documentation or observed enumeration. A missing role line keeps the portable inherit-parent default. A role line of inherit-parent or auto omits explicit model selection. Unsupported configured models or efforts require a disclosed supported fallback, or blocked if that exact choice is required. The source-host named defaults are not portable availability evidence. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified.\n\nWithout an exposed and authorized independent-worker operation, apply the same full-mode and leaf reads sequentially only within authorized scope. Label reduced capability. Sequential roles are not independent workers, independent review, background execution or model diversity. If the playbook requires independence, leave that gate blocked rather than silently waiving it.\n\nA worker forbidden to spawn owns its assigned diff directly and returns it to the lead for review separation, as Feature step 4 requires. It never waits on a nested worker or replies \"standing by\". This exception does not waive the lead's required independent review. Before accepting any result, match assignment identity, exact input revision, full current head SHA, existing artifacts and observed method evidence. Reject stale results and missing artifacts under the assignment contract. Bookkeeping labels cannot satisfy these checks.\n\nYou own every subagent's work. Review the diff and write your own summary, don't pass through what it said. A second opinion is the same prompt against a different model. Treat agreement as supporting evidence only after checking independence, the observed models and the underlying artifacts.\n\n**Fresh subagents by default.** Give new work to a fresh subagent with consolidated scope, meaning the original brief, every later directive, and the prior agent's report and branch. This holds for a fix round, a follow-up, a retry, and the next queue item. Resume, message, or queue a follow-up on an existing subagent only when the new work strictly needs state that lives in that agent and is costly to move: its local checkout, its uncommitted changes, or a process it still runs, such as a dev server, a simulator, or a babysit watcher. A stop or hold order to a running agent is not reuse. A role such as a PR owner outlives its agent. Once that agent returns, a fresh agent takes the role's next round. Interrupt-chained resumes silently drop directives, so fire a fresh subagent with consolidated scope rather than trusting a \"done\" summary.\n\n## Throughput checkpoint\n\nFor nontrivial multi-step work, write these four todo items before fan-out, as required by Feature step 3. Keep an inapplicable dimension with n/a and a reason. Rewrite the checkpoint at phase boundaries and return it with the evidence.\n\n- Blocking first steps. Gates run before fan-out.\n- Independent workstreams. Disjoint files, services or layers parallelize. Shared writes serialize.\n- Shared mutable state. Default to splitting the target using the separate-before-serializing-shared-state principle leaf. Serialize only for real invariants.\n- Smallest safe decomposition. If one worker is best, name why.\n\nCode-coupled work goes to one owner with the checkpoint inline. That owner fans out after the blocking phase when authorized and supported. Parent-level fan-out is for independent artifacts. Assignment records and bookkeeping do not prove workers ran. Keep decision trails in an explicit owned local output path per show-me-your-work and its log helper contract. Missing helper setup or persistence stays visible; do not invent saved logs or cross-session continuity.\n\n## Integrated helper and delivery bindings\n\nRead the selected playbook's complete adapted helper and prompt contracts before dependent execution. The 23 bundled playbook routes below and the Bugbot rubric are local instructions. Historical task deferrals in those files are not proof of absence or permission to skip a present contract. Check the actual path. An absent required contract still blocks its dependent step.\n\n- Decision trails use [show-me-your-work](../show-me-your-work/SKILL.md), its [template](../show-me-your-work/references/decision-log-template.tsv) and [log helper](../show-me-your-work/scripts/log.sh).\n\n  Invoke the helper through authorized execution with exactly `<logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>`.\n\n  The nonempty logfile is an explicit owned local path.\n\n  Tabs, newlines and carriage returns are rejected before writes, and formula-leading cells are quoted.\n\n  Read back the trail and retain the skill's scoped transcript audit, cross-model review and Attention requirements.\n\n  Append-only logging proves neither unattended execution nor recovery.\n- Multi-phase plans use [check-plan.mjs](scripts/check-plan.mjs) as `bun --no-install <package-root>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/check-plan.mjs <plan.md>`.\n\n  It takes one positional path and reads without edits.\n\n  Exit 2 means usage or unreadable input, 1 means format problems, and 0 means structural success.\n\n  Keep capability declarations, live and perf evidence, independent review and exact-head receipts.\n\n  It checks pstack plans only.\n\n  It never validates another controller's plan, implementation or host behavior.\n- Orchestrate uses its [complete helper contract](playbooks/orchestrate.md) and the real [CLI](scripts/orch/orch.ts) and [store](scripts/orch/store.ts).\n\n  Bind `bun --no-install <package-root>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/orch/orch.ts --store <owned-store>` or explicit `ORCH_STORE` outside the package tree.\n\n  Use full JSON for standing orders and rows.\n\n  Preserve `inbox drain --peek --json` and actual drain receipts with durable readback before classification. `ledger check <pr> <full-head-sha>` returns NOT-VERIFIED and exit 2 for a missing key.\n\n  Usage or runtime errors exit 1.\n\n  Ledger rows replace the same key without enforcing verifier precedence.\n\n  The caller owns precedence, append-only attempt history, exact SHA syntax, artifact inspection, terminal states, permissions and current-head filtering.\n\n  The helper never spawns, waits or wakes workers.\n\n  Stale locks require ownership review, and `--force` requires explicit takeover permission even for a dead holder.\n\n  Frontier discovery uses local git and gt in the stack owner's clone and fails on unavailable metadata or pin mismatch.\n\n  Do not substitute this binding for the gt-free Autopilot stack workflow.\n\n  Atomic rename is not a crash-durability or adversarial-concurrency guarantee.\n- Babysit and Shipping use the GitHub-specific read-only [watcher](scripts/watch-pr/watch-pr) through their complete playbooks.\n\n  From the package root bind `bun --no-install skills/poteto-mode/scripts/watch-pr/watch-pr` with explicit `--owner`, `--repo`, PR selection, `--head <number=full-40-character-head>` for every watched member, `--max-polls <remaining-count>` and `--timeout <remaining-seconds>`.\n\n  Freeze queued order with `--queued-stack --stack-prs <bottom-to-top-list>`.\n\n  Connected mode uses `--stack`, mutually exclusive with queued mode. `check` adds `--status-only`.\n\n  Inspect NDJSON schemaVersion 1 kind, terminal and reason.\n\n  Exit 0 includes status-only and proves no readiness by itself.\n\n  Codes 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 and 64 identify conflicts, threads, checks, timeout, merge gates, query failures and usage.\n\n  A changed head, missing authentication or transport, or exhausted poll-budget blocks the result.\n\n  Queued WAITING with reason kind merge-queue is non-terminal.\n\n  Babysit reports the frontier merge-ready and stops that watcher.\n\n  Shipping waits for observed merge under its distinct rule.\n\n  Carry budgets across re-arms and retain an observed stop or deadline binding for a hard wall-clock limit.\n\n  The helper deadline is checked between polling work.\n\n  Do not add a second sleep loop or treat GitHub output as Origin evidence.\n- Cleanup uses the [audit wrapper](scripts/worktree-audit.sh) and [implementation](scripts/worktree-audit.py) through its playbook with an explicit local `--repo` and verified `--base`, plus authorized `--history-map` and `--pr-state` exports when available. The helper returns logical bytes and evidence buckets, performs no fetch or deletion, and leaves unknown facts as holds. A bucket never grants deletion permission. Recheck actual usage and ignored files before separately authorized removal.\n\nThe [bootstrap](scripts/bootstrap.ts), [manifest](scripts/package.json) and [lock](scripts/bun.lock) require exact commander 14.0.0, bun-types 1.3.14 and typescript 7.0.2 metadata before dependency-backed CLI execution. Bootstrap fails setup-required and never installs. Run helpers with Bun runtime auto-install disabled. Missing dependencies remain blocked. The isolated tested snapshot only supports that snapshot, not this caller, the current checkout or either host. [Helper setup](../../docs/testing/helper-setup.md) states the exact-copy validation boundary and retains historical setup evidence. It does not authorize installation or transfer a historical pass to this caller. Any future setup needs fresh authorization for an exact writable copy, registry, disabled lifecycle scripts and task-owned cache. Never install in an operative invocation or bypass bootstrap.\n\nDelivery reads [Opening a PR](playbooks/opening-a-pr.md) in full and retains its review, companion, commit, push, draft or ready authorization and actual-result gates. A local draft is not a created PR. An exposed built-in PR tool owns operations it covers, and a failed tool does not authorize a CLI bypass. Opening alone never starts Babysit. A read-only question stays in Investigation, with no PR; its specific exception takes precedence over the generic delivery route. Prototype and planning handoffs grant no implementation or external permission. Missing create-skill, deslop, matching controls or independent review remains an explicit blocked gate even when a reduced local artifact is useful.\n\n## Writing the reply\n\nWrite the reply clean as you draft it. A cleanup pass after drafting does not remove these patterns.\n\n- **Short declarative sentences.** One thought per sentence, ended with a period.\n- **No long-dash character anywhere.** Write a file-list bullet as a sentence (\"`main.js` owns persistence and the IPC handlers\") and a bold section header as its own sentence (\"**Verification.** End to end via CDP\").\n- **A colon as a mid-sentence connector is also out** (unslop rule 14). A colon before a list is fine.\n- **Terse is not an excuse to drop content.** Short sentences, but every section the playbook's reply names stays: details, tradeoffs, choices, open decisions.\n- **Frame impact for the consumer and the maintainer.** Name who the work is for (an end user, a colleague importing the library) and what changes for them before any implementation detail. Then what the next engineer who owns this code inherits. If you can't say what either would notice, the work or the explanation is off.\n- **Never fabricate a link, citation, or transcript reference.** Link only artifacts you produced or read this session.\n- **Every claim carries its evidence or its label in the same sentence.** Measured, inferred, or guess. A prediction or an unseen cause is a guess. Never hand the human a check you could run.\n\nEvery playbook ends with a reply written this way. Link to the actual PR you read or created when authorized. Never claim a draft is a created PR. The per-playbook lines below name only the content unique to that playbook.\n\n## Comments\n\nComments follow the same rule as the reply. Write them clean as you go. Keep a comment only for a non-obvious *why* the code can't show. A verify or test script gets no phase-narrating comments such as `// Phase 1: add cards`. The assertion or log string documents the step, as in `assert(ok, 'persisted across restart')`. This applies to every file you produce, including the delegate's diff.\n\n## Playbooks\n\nOpen an available todo list, or an authorized local Markdown checklist if no todo operation exists. Record that reduced tool binding. Its first items are the matched playbook's steps, copied in verbatim, before any task-specific todos. A step you choose not to do stays in the list with a one-line `skip: <reason>`. Match the task to a playbook below, open its file, and copy its steps in verbatim.\n\nA large or cross-cutting effort (a migration across many call sites, an ambitious multi-part change), or work the user steps away from to trust later, routes to the **figure-it-out** skill even when a narrower playbook like Feature fits. Use **figure-it-out** whenever no bundled playbook fits. It designs a bespoke, rigorous playbook for the task. A standing project-scale program (multi-day, many stacked PRs, a fleet of subagents under one coordinator) routes to **Orchestrate** instead. figure-it-out designs one bespoke run, orchestrate runs the program.\n\n- **Investigation.** Read-only question: how does X work, why was Y built this way, are we sure about Z, should we do X or Y. `playbooks/investigation.md`.\n- **Bug fix.** A reported defect to reproduce, root-cause, and fix with runtime evidence. `playbooks/bug-fix.md`.\n- **Perf issue.** A measured slowness to trace and improve against a baseline. `playbooks/perf-issue.md`.\n- **Hillclimb.** Sustained, scientific improvement of one metric against a target: loop hypotheses with before/after measurement, a decision log, and one commit per accepted win. Distinct from Perf issue, which is a one-off fix. `playbooks/hillclimb.md`.\n- **Runtime forensics.** Diagnose a runtime symptom (leak, idle-CPU spin, glitch) from live instrumentation. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix. `playbooks/runtime-forensics.md`.\n- **Trace forensics.** Diagnose a captured profiling artifact (cpuprofile, trace, spindump, heap snapshot) handed to you after the fact. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix. `playbooks/trace-forensics.md`.\n- **Feature.** New or changed behavior, built from a named data shape. `playbooks/feature.md`.\n- **Refactoring.** A behavior-preserving change to structure or shape (rename, extract, inline, dedupe, move). `playbooks/refactoring.md`.\n- **Prototype.** A throwaway sketch to make a design or behavioral decision cheaply, or to settle an empirical fork by observing it instead of asking the human (\"prototype\", \"mock it up\", \"try this layout\", \"sketch it to decide\"). `playbooks/prototype.md`.\n- **Visual parity.** Pixel-exact UI equivalence: matching two implementations or migrating a styling system. `playbooks/visual-parity.md`.\n- **Authoring or modifying a skill.** Writing or editing a SKILL.md. `playbooks/authoring-a-skill.md`.\n- **Eval.** Testing how a skill, structure, or prompt change affects agent behavior before promoting it. `playbooks/eval.md`.\n- **Babysit.** Driving a PR or a stack to merge-ready: conflicts, review threads, CI. `playbooks/babysit.md`.\n- **Shipping.** The half after Babysit. Independently verifying a green stack, then landing the contiguous verified run bottom-up through `gh` by default or Origin when its CLI is available. `playbooks/shipping.md`.\n- **Autonomous run.** A long task to drive to completion without stopping (\"run until done\", \"/loop until X\"). `playbooks/autonomous-run.md`.\n- **Orchestrate.** A standing project handed to one coordinator chat: multi-day, many stacked PRs, dozens to hundreds of subagents, minimal human turns (\"run this whole project\", \"own this migration until it lands\"). Distinct from Autonomous run, which drives one task to a predicate. Work one agent could finish inside the session's budget routes there, not here, however program-shaped the phrasing sounds. `playbooks/orchestrate.md`.\n- **Autopilot-full.** A queue of independent PRs run to merged with full autonomy. One owner per PR carries build through merge, and the root swarm-verifies each PR before its owner merges (\"autopilot this queue\", \"full autopilot\", one-owner-per-PR programs). `playbooks/autopilot-full.md`.\n- **Autopilot-stack.** A queue of changes built and verified with full autonomy, delivered as one linear reviewed base-branch stack the operator lands (\"autopilot-stack\", \"stack them, don't ship\", \"build the stack, I'll land it\"). `playbooks/autopilot-stack.md`.\n- **Session pickup.** Resuming or taking over a prior agent's in-flight work from a transcript, cloud-agent URL, or pushed branch. `playbooks/session-pickup.md`.\n- **Pause safely.** Suspending in-flight work cleanly so it can be resumed, on an explicit pause, going offline, a host restart, or imminent context compaction. The complement to Session pickup. Full steps: `playbooks/pause-safely.md`.\n- **Multi-phase or multi-PR plan.** Work that spans phases or stacked PRs. `playbooks/multi-phase-plan.md`.\n- **Worktree and simulator cleanup.** Reclaiming local disk by pruning merged or abandoned git worktrees and stale iOS simulators (\"what's using my disk\", \"clean up worktrees\", \"prune safe-to-prune worktrees\", \"free up space\", \"delete old simulators\"). `playbooks/worktree-cleanup.md`.\n- **Opening a PR.** Invoked at the end of every other playbook. `playbooks/opening-a-pr.md`.\n"

      "text": "---\nname: principle-prove-it-works\ndescription: \"Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.'\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Prove It Works\n\nVerify every task output by checking the real thing directly. Do not infer from proxies, self-reports, or \"it compiles.\"\n\n**Why:** Unverified work has unknown correctness. Indirect verification (file mtimes, output freshness, agent self-reports, cached screenshots) feels cheaper than direct observation. Acting on a wrong inference costs far more than checking the source.\n\nCheck the real thing, not a proxy:\n- Check process liveness directly, not indirectly through derived state\n- Read the actual value, not a cached or derived representation\n- When verification fails, suspect the observation method before suspecting the system\n\n## Script the check when you can\n\nThe strongest proof is a deterministic script that re-runs the same comparison, not a one-time eyeball. Write the script, run it, and keep its output as an artifact a reviewer can re-run instead of trusting your word.\n\nKeep the artifact visible for the human. Commit it only for large or complex work where the trail has to be auditable later, like a big port or migration (the **show-me-your-work** skill).\n\n## Session capabilities and permissions\n\nUse the exposed tools and observed operations described in [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Run checks and retain artifacts only within the user's authorized scope and host permissions. If direct inspection or execution is unavailable or denied, state which outputs remain unverified and withhold completion for those outputs. A proposed script is not evidence that it ran.\n"

    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-11.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-11.txt",
      "sha256": "483e5d66b0ec7fcbf98fe7175c20d60109f6a35c3c7fc8cc34771f42fee20083",

      "text": "---\nname: principle-build-the-lever\ndescription: \"Apply to any non-trivial work, not just bulk work: edits, migrations, analyses, checks. Build the tool that does it or proves it (codemod, script, generator, or a skill your subagents follow) instead of working by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer can rerun.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n# Build the Lever\n\nWhen the work isn't trivial, build the tool that does it instead of doing it by hand.\n\n**Why:** Two payoffs. Throughput: a codemod, generator, or script does the work the same way every time and reruns for free. Confidence: the tool is one artifact a reviewer can read and rerun to check the work. Hand-done changes can only be re-verified by redoing them. A deterministic script turns \"trust me\" into \"run this\".\n\n**Pattern:** Default to building the lever. Skip it only when the task is trivial, a couple of obvious edits you can see at a glance.\n\n- Do the first unit by hand to learn the recipe, then build the tool. Prove it by rerunning it on that unit and diffing against your hand-done version. Make the lever safe to rerun.\n- Codemod or script for edits, generator for repetitive files, a dump-to-sqlite query for analysis, a rerunnable check for verification.\n- A deterministic lever beats fan-out. If the tool can process every unit in one pass, run it yourself. Don't fan out delegates to hand-apply what a script can do.\n- When you fan work out to subagents, write the lever as a skill they all read: the recipe, the verification contract, and the do-not-touch fences in one artifact. Keep it outside the delegates' write scope so they can't quietly edit the contract.\n- Applying this principle produces a file. If you cited it and there is no codemod, script, generator, or delegate skill in the diff, you didn't apply it.\n- Commit the lever when the work outlives the session.\n\n**Balance:** The bar is triviality, not repetition. A one-off still earns a lever when the lever is what makes the work checkable. Per the [Laziness Protocol](../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md), build the smallest script that does or proves the job, never a framework.\n\nDistinct from [Encode Lessons in Structure](../principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md), which makes a recurring instruction a durable guardrail. This is throughput and reviewability on the work in front of you. For scripting the verification itself, see [Prove It Works](../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md).\n\n## Host capability and permission boundaries\n\nUse the [host capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to discover file reads, authorized writes, execution and persistence. A proposed tool is not a saved artifact. A saved artifact is not executed proof. Report the commands, exit status and comparisons you observed; if execution is denied or unavailable, state that proof is blocked and provide the proposed rerun and diff procedure as reduced capability. If source reads are unavailable, do not claim a verified recipe.\n\nDelegation requires user authorization and exposed independent-worker operations under the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Keep the shared recipe outside worker write scopes. Sequential work is reduced capability and never proves independent workers. Preserve the [active workflow owner](../../references/workflow-ownership.md).\n\nRetaining the lever beyond the session does not grant commit permission. Commit only when authorized. Do not install dependencies, publish or perform external writes as incidental steps. Preserve `disable-model-invocation: true` metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-12.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-12.txt",
      "sha256": "b0b6016d092279a26a0984adc4c601ee3c0d4e58787f4438ad9e0b5ada9e5e22",
      "bytes": 1987,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "---\nname: principle-guard-the-context-window\ndescription: \"Apply when context is filling up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents; keep summaries in the main thread, not raw payloads.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Guard the Context Window\n\nThe context window is finite and non-renewable within a session. Every token should be worth its cost.\n\n**Why:** Context overflow degrades reasoning quality, creates compression artifacts, and halts progress.\n\n**Pattern:**\n- **Isolate large payloads.** Route verbose outputs, screenshots, and large documents to subagents. The main context gets summaries, not raw data.\n- **Keep frequently used content inline.** Templates and references used on every invocation belong in the skill file, not in separate files that cost a read each time.\n- **Size phases and cap scope.** Limit files per phase, set turn budgets, account for mechanism costs.\n\n## Capability and permission boundaries\n\nUse the [session capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to check evidence access and independent-worker operations. Delegate only when the task authorizes it and the session exposes those operations. Follow the [assignment and integration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), retain observed worker identities and inspect their results before reporting summaries. This principle grants no additional permissions.\n\nIf independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, narrow accessible evidence into bounded sequential phases. Keep summaries in the main thread and label this reduced capability. Sequential work does not establish worker independence. If required evidence is inaccessible, name the missing input and access limit; keep the plan provisional and do not claim to have read or summarized it.\n\nPreserve the invocation metadata above without assuming the host enforces it. Report delegation and inspection only after observing the authorized operations.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-13.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-13.txt",
      "sha256": "9ef714543b16665c68182e1b66beb22829fbc4b68e2ceb138db9858384c598ce",
      "bytes": 2264,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "---\nname: principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state\ndescription: \"Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key, or state object. Eliminate the sharing first; serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Separate Before Serializing Shared State\n\nWhen concurrent actors might share mutable state, first ask whether they need the same mutable object. If not, eliminate the sharing. When sharing is real, enforce serialization structurally: lockfiles, sequential phases, exclusive ownership. Instructions and conventions are not concurrency control.\n\n**Why:** Concurrent writes to shared state create race conditions that are intermittent, hard to reproduce, and expensive to debug.\n\n**Pattern:**\n1. **Identify shared mutable state** (files both read and write, branches both push to, APIs both define and consume).\n2. **Default: eliminate the shared write target.** Ask: do these actors need one canonical object, or are they publishing independent facts? Give each actor its own owned file, key, branch, or state directory, and merge only at the read/reporting boundary. Two workers writing their own `lastX` field into one `state.json` is still shared mutation. `indexer-state.json` + `metrics-state.json` is not.\n3. **Only when one shared write target is a real invariant, serialize access structurally** (lockfiles, sequential phases, single-writer actor, or atomic compare-and-swap). Treat \"we need a lock\" as a design smell to check, not as the default answer.\n\n## Session capabilities and permissions\n\nBefore dispatching writers or changing shared state, check [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) and follow the [assignment and integration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Assign distinct owned paths before writes start. Isolation does not grant permission to spawn workers, write files or perform external actions.\n\nIf execution or writes are unavailable or denied, state the missing requirement and provide the isolation design without claiming implementation. Authorized sequential work is reduced capability. A design or sequential run does not prove independent workers or prevention of concurrent races.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-14.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-14.txt",
      "sha256": "5818e68d1e41af2143362a3126b36ea6c15596b28a6e3a3235390c73cf4398a3",
      "bytes": 2448,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "---\nname: principle-sequence-verifiable-units\ndescription: \"Apply to multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and to how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a verifiable state, check each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself to a reviewer.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Sequence work into verifiable units\n\nOrder work as a sequence of small units, each ending in a state you can check, and don't advance until the current one is green.\n\n**Why:** A break caught at the unit that caused it is cheap to localize. A break caught after a batch is buried, and you have already built further on a broken base. Sequencing those same units into a delivery a reviewer can replay turns \"trust me\" into \"watch it go red, then green.\"\n\n**Execution.** In a sweep, migration, or any run of similar edits, verify each change before starting the next. Each unit is a before/after bracket: known-good state, one change, run the check, then proceed. Rebase onto clean trunk first so every check measures against the real baseline. When a lever does the edits, the per-unit check is nearly free. Run it anyway.\n\n**Delivery.** Stack commits and PRs in the order that proves the work. The canonical shape is the failing test first, then the fix on top. Other story orders are a subtraction before the reshape, a baseline capture before the treatment, the scaffold before the feature. Each commit lands on its own and the sequence reads as an argument.\n\nThe sequencing complement to the **prove-it-works** principle skill, which keeps each check real, and the **build-the-lever** principle skill, which makes the per-unit check cheap.\n\n## Capability and authorization boundary\n\nUse [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to establish available inspection and execution, and the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md) for write ownership and authorization. Rebase onto clean trunk only when authorized and safe for the current work. Commits and PRs also require authorization and an available operation. If a required check cannot run, state that it remains unverified and block advancement; an unrun check is never green. A reduced workflow may provide the ordered units, specific checks and delivery plan while identifying unperformed operations. Preserve `disable-model-invocation` metadata without assuming host enforcement.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-15.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-15.txt",
      "sha256": "0c5116c90901bb8efc6e7b44f170bc9b7dd1a55b6cb2fa9f3abfd600d5c905fe",
      "bytes": 1825,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "---\nname: principle-laziness-protocol\ndescription: \"Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Laziness Protocol\n\nAim for the most result with the least code and complexity.\n\n- **Prefer deletion.** When asked to refactor or improve, look for removals before additions.\n- **Maintain a flat call hierarchy.** Avoid deep call chains. A rich interface that hides substantial work is not a deep call chain. If answering a question requires tracing through more than 3 files or layers, flatten it.\n- **Consolidate decisions.** Do not repeat the same choice in several places. Put it behind one source of truth and pass the result as a simple flag.\n- **Minimize the diff.** Make the smallest change that solves the problem. Fewer lines beat \"elegant\" boilerplate.\n- **Question the threading.** If a task asks you to pass a new signal through types, schemas, pipelines, or similar layers, stop and look for a more direct path.\n- **Sweat the small leaks.** Remove tiny pass-throughs, representation leaks, and duplicated choices before they spread. Small leaks compound into permanent coordination costs.\n\n**The test:** If a human developer would find the code exhausting to maintain, it is a bad solution.\n\n## Capability limits\n\nApply this principle within the authorized task. Follow [session capability checks](../../references/host-capabilities.md) before claiming file reads, edits or verification. When writes or execution are unavailable, label the read-only proposal as reduced capability and state the missing checks. If required source is unavailable, state that the refactor cannot be assessed. Structural validation does not prove runtime behavior.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-16.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-16.txt",
      "sha256": "d4e8519780ffeb2f0dea45b0c2fa92051c780666eba074f0033eef99875c8536",
      "bytes": 5125,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "### Perf issue\n\nYou own the measurement story. Plan, review, verify the numbers. Tie every fix to a measurement, don't read source instead of measuring.\n\nRead the complete [poteto mode](../SKILL.md) and required leaves. Use [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md), the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md) and the full [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md). Record actual exposed controls, execution and artifact access before using them. Missing required reads stop dependent work. Do not install controls or profilers implicitly.\n\n1. Capture a baseline trace via the matching control skill. Discover the actual control-cli or control-ui companion and its exposed tools for the measured CLI, UI or runtime. Missing or denied matching controls leave capture blocked. Reviewing supplied traces is a reduced assessment and does not prove a new capture. Vet the baseline, and each later number, with the complete [benchmark-checklist](../../benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md) and its [explain-the-number](../../principle-explain-the-number/SKILL.md) contract. Record the exact baseline and treatment revisions, workload, data size, concurrency, timed region, configuration, tuning, cache state, warmup, sample count, errors and completed work. Keep comparable runs and their artifacts. A choice between fixes is not the checklist's one-run ballpark exception.\n2. Run [how](../../how/SKILL.md) to ground hypotheses. Don't claim a perf ceiling without running it first.\n   Try the performance mantras in order, cheapest first:\n   1. Don't do it. Stop work whose result nothing uses rather than cheapening it.\n   2. Do it, but don't do it again.\n   3. Do it less.\n   4. Do it later.\n   5. Do it when they're not looking.\n   6. Do it concurrently.\n   7. Do it cheaper.\n\n   When an earlier mantra meets the target, stop.\n3. Plan the fix from the trace. If it crosses a function boundary, run the complete [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) workflow first, including its runner prompts, candidate screening and rationale. Delegate implementation to an authorized independent worker with the complete poteto role prompt, an exact input revision and distinct owned output. Use the configured perf-issue role only when its model and effort are supported by current exposed operations. Otherwise request inherited selection and disclose rejected or unavailable selections. Record requested and observed values separately; unknown observed values prove no model diversity. If the exact selection is required but unavailable, block it. Inspect the returned artifacts and review the diff. Without authorized workers, perform authorized sequential implementation as reduced capability; independent delegation remains unsatisfied. Capture a post-fix trace on the matching measured system and vet it with the same checklist.\n   Apply [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md), verifying each attempt before trying the next. An unavailable check blocks advancement and stays unverified.\n4. Parse and compare the artifacts, including JSON to sqlite and a diff when applicable, using available authorized execution and storage. Retain original traces and the comparison artifact. Apply all seven checklist questions. For a comparison, run each side at least five times, alternate the sides, report median and range, and treat a gap smaller than variation as no measurable difference. Keep profiling runs separate from reported timings. Name the limiter from run evidence, check physical limits, production tuning, correct outputs, error counts, actual completed work and end-to-end relevance. \"Inconclusive\" or wrong-surface is not a pass. Flag it. Missing limiter, an untuned side, or unchecked errors or completed work makes a claimed difference inconclusive. Incompatible workloads, revisions or configurations cannot support the proposed performance claim.\n5. Cite the measurement in the PR. Keep one primary number with its unit in the body and link the runs, range, limiter and trace comparison artifact. Prepare a local draft when PR writes are unauthorized; do not claim that draft is a created PR.\n6. Run the complete [Opening a PR](opening-a-pr.md) playbook only within actual authorization and exposed capabilities. Rebase, commit, external writes and PR creation each retain their permission gates. Missing delivery dependencies stop delivery-dependent work. Opening a PR does not start Babysit.\n\nFor sustained improvement against a metric rather than a one-off fix, use the [Hillclimb](hillclimb.md) playbook. Preserve this playbook's earlier-mantra stop rule for the one-off fix. Hillclimb and Opening a PR remain pending companion dependencies for tasks 18 and 21, with task 24 integration.\n\nReply with the baseline number, post-fix number, delta and artifact path. Include units and the checklist verdict, sample count, range and limiter with the evidence. Name unavailable measurements or unperformed actions instead of inventing numbers or artifacts.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-17.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-17.txt",
      "sha256": "d7a61255568f81fb8facddc8d8f5aaa710ce0407569175eccdf7dd050744ba1a",
      "bytes": 5428,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "### Autonomous run\n\nOwn the exit condition within the authorized scope. Define done, then drive to it while execution is available. This is the playbook for \"run until done\" and \"/loop until X\". The source's /loop was a host built-in, not a pstack skill or a portable tool binding.\n\nRead [poteto-mode](../SKILL.md) in full. Discover actual operations through [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md) and follow the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Upstream autonomy does not override user gates or host permissions.\n\n1. State the exit condition as a checkable predicate before the first iteration, such as tests green, repro fixed, all N PRs merged or pixel-diff zero. Record the check and its current evidence. A merge predicate does not authorize merging.\n2. Pick an exposed, authorized wake mechanism and observe its registration before claiming it active. An event to watch, such as CI, a merge or a ref advancing, gets an independent watcher worker with a long time-based heartbeat as fallback. No event gets a fixed-interval heartbeat sized to when the result is worth re-checking. Use the [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md), exact assignment revision and isolated outputs for a watcher. Follow [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md), defaulting to inherit-parent and recording actual models only when observed. Record missing or failed wake, heartbeat, worker and stop/control bindings separately. Never install or schedule implicitly. If persistent wake is unavailable, continue useful authorized iterations in the current turn and save the continuation brief below before returning. Label this reduced capability, without a promise of work after the turn. A foreground check is not an independent watcher. If control is unavailable, disclose that limit and do not claim a worker stopped or unattended recovery is safe.\n3. Each iteration makes the smallest change the evidence justifies and verifies it against the predicate. Commit if it advanced and commit authorization and review gates are satisfied. Otherwise keep the verified change local and report the unmet gate. Discard changes that did not help, reverting only this run's owned changes. Revert speculative fixes that merely \"might help\". Sequence work through [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md), verifying each unit before the next instead of batching checks at the end. Missing verification blocks dependent advancement.\n4. Address mid-run broken skills, related bugs, flaky verifiers, review noise, tooling failures, orphaned follow-ups and fixable drift through poteto-mode within authorization. Put out-of-band fixes in their own PR when PR creation is authorized, using the mode's Opening a PR route. Otherwise prepare the separate local fix and disclose the unperformed PR action. Do not park authorized reversible work for the human or ask a question an experiment can settle. Surface irreversible actions, explicit operator gates, genuine product or preference calls no experiment can settle, or a real dead end. Stop dependent work on a broken contract, continue independent work, and return to the main predicate after each side fix.\n5. Checkpoint every iteration through [show-me-your-work](../../show-me-your-work/SKILL.md), with what changed and whether the predicate moved. Use its [log helper](../../show-me-your-work/scripts/log.sh) through authorized execution with an explicit owned local log path and its [template](../../show-me-your-work/references/decision-log-template.tsv). Keep append-only history, read back saved records, audit against this run's available transcript and preserve its cross-model review and Attention requirements. Missing history, persistence or independent review remains visible. A saved log does not prove unattended execution.\n6. Stop successfully only when the predicate is met. A plateau is not completion. Keep going and pivot while authorization and execution permit. Surface a genuine dead end instead of spinning and never relax the predicate to declare victory. A session boundary, unavailable persistent tool or explicit pause requires a handoff with the predicate still open, not a completion claim.\n\nWhen ongoing work cannot survive the turn, save a continuation brief at an explicit authorized local path and read it back. Include the unchanged exit predicate and current state, iterations completed, current revision and owned changes, verification commands and results, decision-log path, accepted and discarded changes, outstanding gates and missing capabilities, pending action identities and known outcomes, and the next check or experiment. A new run must inspect current state and permissions before continuing or replaying an action. Unknown external outcomes block replay. If local persistence is denied or unavailable, return the brief inline and state that no durable save occurred. Neither form registers a wake mechanism or proves automatic pickup.\n\nReply with the exit condition, iterations run, what landed, what was discarded and final predicate state. Distinguish verified local work from actual commits, PRs and merges. Link the saved trail and continuation brief only after readback. Report missing capabilities, blocked gates and any unperformed action. Keep show-me-your-work's Attention section when a trail was produced.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-18.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-18.txt",
      "sha256": "4198f32b22cf1d8ff367deee1dbfea805e6eecdd7ff1da3409ae7c0b07fb1c59",
      "bytes": 8516,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "### Opening a PR\n\nInvoked at the end of every other playbook.\n\nUse [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), [workflow ownership](../../../references/workflow-ownership.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md). These documents live at the package root. Prepare a reviewable title, body, verification artifacts, and intended base before any external operation. Record explicit authorization, the repository, branch head, destination and action identity. Check the destination after an interrupted action before any replay. Missing access or permission leaves the external action blocked and the content available locally. Draft-only permission permits a draft, never readiness, push, merge or comments.\n\n**Worktree.** Work from a git worktree off main. Subagents inherit it. Multiple independent workers on the same branch each get their own isolated worktree. Preserve unrelated dirty work before creating an authorized fresh worktree. Report a snarled worktree instead of resetting or discarding unowned changes. Missing independent workers permits an authorized sequential reduction, never a claim of independent review.\n\n**Commits.** Commit liberally within the authorized local scope. Rebase into small, ordered commits before opening PRs. Each commit is a future PR: landable, ordered to tell the story. Amend when the fix belongs in a just-made commit. New commit when separable.\n\n**PRs.** Run `/deslop` over the diff before commit if that external skill is exposed and authorized. It is not bundled here. If absent, disclose the missing slop-strip and leave that gate open and block creation until the required review gate is satisfied; do not install it or treat prose cleanup as equivalent. Run `/no-comments` through [no-comments](../../no-comments/SKILL.md) before review. Write every PR title, PR description, and commit body with `/technical-writing` through [technical-writing](../../technical-writing/SKILL.md), then apply `/unslop` through [unslop](../../unslop/SKILL.md). Apply every technical-writing layer except Diátaxis. Use one word for each action, keep articles, and avoid `-ing` when a plain verb works.\n\n**Titles.** Use Conventional Commits in the form `type(scope): subject`. Use `feat`, `fix`, `docs`, `refactor`, `test`, `chore`, or `perf` as the type. Use the changed area, such as `pstack` or `poteto-mode`, as the scope. Keep the subject short and imperative. Name a real symbol when one carries the change. For example, `fix(pstack): retarget opening-a-pr babysit trigger`. Do not add a trailing period.\n\n**Descriptions.** The PR body is a briefing, not the lab notebook. A reviewer who has the diff should learn why the change exists, what it leaves out, what it could break, and how you proved it works, in under a minute. Write short, simple sentences with few identifiers. Do not write walls of text. The squash commit body is the PR body. If the body would make the squash commit longer than about 40 lines, cut the body.\n\nPut each section under a `##` heading, not a bold lead-in, so the sections stand apart. Use these sections in order. Drop a section when it has nothing to say.\n\n- `## Why` gives the problem and the approach in one to three short sentences. Do not list SHAs or rebase genealogy. Do not add a \"based on main\" preamble.\n- `## What changed` has one to three short bullets. Name a real symbol or path only when it carries the change. Name both sides of a rename or retarget.\n- `## Scope` always names what the PR covers and what it deliberately leaves out, for example a related follow-up or a known gap. Use one to three short items. Do not list symbols or paths, and do not write a file-by-file essay.\n- `## Tradeoffs` names only rejected alternatives that a reviewer would otherwise ask about. Skip this section when there was no real choice.\n- `## Blast Radius` gives one or two sentences on who or what the change touches and why that is safe or risky. If main is red, state the cost of leaving it red.\n- `## Verification` has one to three bullets. Each bullet names a real run path and its outcome. For a performance change, report one primary number with its unit in `before → after` form. Link the arena or swarm directory for the remaining evidence. Do not include sample-size methodology, swarm recitals, or metric tables.\n\nAfter these sections, attach videos or screenshots when they prove a claim. Do not paste full SHAs, swarm or arena lane recitals, lever-correction essays, file-by-file checklists, or \"CLEAN\" verdicts. Put these details in a linked artifact. A commit body does not restate its subject.\n\n**Forge.** Resolve the forge before the first PR operation and keep that choice for create, edit, view, watch, and merge. GitHub CLI (`gh`) is the default. If `command -v origin` succeeds and Origin can resolve the repository, prefer `origin pr ...`. If Origin is absent or cannot resolve the repository, stay on `gh` and record the fallback. Do not require Graphite (`gt`).\n\n**Built-in PR tool.** When the run provides a built-in PR tool, create, edit, retarget, and mark ready through it, never through a forge CLI. Its exposed instructions define the actual interface. An unavailable, denied or failed tool is a stated blocker, not permission to bypass its tracking through a CLI. A PR made with the CLI misses what the tool tracks, such as a description later runs can edit. Use the resolved forge for everything the tool does not cover, and for every PR operation when the run has no such tool.\n\n**Size and stacks.** Prefer five narrow PRs to one large PR. A stack is a base-branch chain. The root PR targets trunk. Each child branch rebases onto its parent's exact tip and its PR targets the parent branch. Without a built-in PR tool, create a child with `origin pr create --status open --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr create --base <parent-branch>` according to the resolved forge, and retarget an existing child with `origin pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>` or `gh pr edit <pr> --base <parent-branch>`. Branch from trunk only for independent work. Rebase on trunk before substantial stack work.\n\n**Readiness.** The source workflow opens every PR ready, never as a draft, when readiness is explicitly authorized. If only draft creation is authorized, preserve that limit and create a draft through the exposed tool or `gh pr create --draft`. Verify the actual draft state and report the readiness gate open. Use Origin draft creation only when its current interface supports it. Otherwise report blocked. All readiness instructions below require readiness authorization. A built-in PR tool can default to draft, so use its observed ready-creation interface, such as `draft: false` only when that field is actually supported. With Origin, pass `--status open`. With `gh`, omit `--draft`. If a PR still opens as a draft, mark it ready through the PR tool, or run `origin pr ready <number>` or `gh pr ready <number>` according to the resolved forge. Run `origin pr view <number>` or `gh pr view <number>` before you refer to PR status.\n\n**Babysit.** Opening a PR does not start a babysit. Post the URL and keep building. Finish the phase or stack first. Run a separate babysit pass only when the user asks for one after the whole stack exists. A babysit for each new PR stalls the build and spends checks on commits that later waves restart. Push back when feedback drifts from intent.\n\nA subagent that opens a PR runs `interrogate` through [interrogate](../../interrogate/SKILL.md), `/deslop`, and `/no-comments`, and posts the URL. Then it returns to the parent without babysitting, unless it is an Autopilot-full or Autopilot-stack owner. That owner's brief assigns the babysit loop and is the ask [babysit](babysit.md) waits for. The owner starts the loop after its code-ready report and reports merge-ready or STACK-READY as its playbook says. The rules here and in [babysit](babysit.md) that hold babysitting until a whole stack is built do not apply to that owner.\n\nFor the owner exception, read [Autopilot-full](autopilot-full.md) or [Autopilot-stack](autopilot-stack.md) and the [Bugbot triage rubric](../references/bugbot-triage.md). These briefs assign babysitting only within explicit user authorization and observed capabilities. Opening a PR alone never supplies that authorization. No watcher, bootstrap or orchestration helper runs in this opening step. Babysit owns the actual watcher binding.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-19.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-19.txt",
