      "text": "# Poteto agent prompt\n\nUse this role for `/poteto-mode` and requests for poteto's style. Supply these instructions to a fresh independent worker for each new task when the session exposes and permits that operation. Resume a worker only in the strict cases named by poteto-mode's Subagents section. Generic worker routing is acceptable only when it carries this entire prompt and the required mode read; otherwise it skips the style contract.\n\nBefore any work, read [poteto-mode's SKILL.md](../../skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md) in full, including its inline Principles index. Navigate to the leaf `principle-*` skill whenever you apply that principle. If the mode or an applicable leaf is unavailable, report that missing dependency and stop dependent work. Do not reconstruct the mode from a summary.\n\nFollow [model roles](../model-roles.md) and [session capabilities](../host-capabilities.md). This prompt does not register an agent or promise background execution. Record requested and observed models separately. Preserve user authorization and host permissions throughout the mode. An authorized sequential fallback uses the same full read and discloses reduced capability; it does not count as an independent worker.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-2.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-2.txt",
      "sha256": "bca7b43af6d6d0089d20edb2ec2178d681d589357c20ea345eddbf81ce87f7e8",
      "bytes": 4092,

        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "---\nname: how\ndescription: \"Use for \\\"how does X work\\\", code walkthroughs before changing something, and placement / ownership / layering questions (\\\"where should this live\\\", \\\"which package owns this\\\", \\\"is this the right layer\\\"). Explains subsystem architecture, runtime flow, onboarding mental models. Use why for motivation.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# How\n\nExplore the codebase to answer \"how does X work?\" questions. Produce architectural explanations at the level of a senior engineer onboarding onto a subsystem, enough to build a working mental model, not so much that it reads like annotated source code.\n\nBefore dispatch, read [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Bind worker operations to tools actually exposed and authorized in this session. Request the inherited parent model and effort unless a supported explicit selection is observed. Retain rejections and disclose replacements; an unavailable required selection is blocked. Record requested and observed selections separately, using unknown for unobserved values.\n\nKeep explorers and explainers read-only. Worker assignments record their role, distinct angle, exact input revision, identity and inspected results under the assignment contract. Reading a prompt does not register a worker. If independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, disclose reduced capability and perform the same exploration and explanation roles sequentially. Never claim parallel or independent exploration from sequential work. If source access is unavailable or denied, report the gap and limit the explanation to inspected evidence.\n\n## Step 1. Assess Complexity\n\nIf the scope is ambiguous, state your interpretation and explore. The user can redirect.\n\n- **Simple** (a single module, a small utility, a narrow question such as \"how does function X work\"): no explorers. One explainer explores and explains in a single pass. Go to Step 2b.\n- **Complex** (a subsystem spanning multiple files or services, a cross-cutting feature, a full architectural overview): use parallel explorers when available, then hand off to the explainer. Go to Step 2a.\n\nWhen in doubt, take the simple path.\n\n## Step 2a. Explore (complex questions only)\n\nDecompose the question into 2 to 4 exploration angles, each a distinct slice of the subsystem. Dispatch all explorers together through an observed worker binding, with distinct assignments:\n\n- Role: how explorer\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\n- Access: read-only\n\nEach explorer gets the prompt in `references/explorer-prompt.md` with its angle filled in. If workers are unavailable, explore those distinct angles sequentially and retain separate findings. Then go to Step 3.\n\n## Step 2b. Direct Explain (simple questions)\n\nAssign one explainer worker that explores and explains in one pass:\n\n- Role: how explainer\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\n- Access: read-only\n\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` without the explorer-findings section. The explainer reads and traces the source itself. If workers are unavailable, apply this role sequentially. Go to Step 4.\n\n## Step 3. Synthesize (complex questions only)\n\nOnce all explorer results have been inspected, assign one explainer worker to synthesize their findings into one explanation:\n\n- Role: how explainer\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\n- Access: read-only\n\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` with every explorer's findings filled in. In reduced capability, synthesize the sequential findings under the same read-only role.\n\n## Step 4. Present\n\nPresent the explainer's output to the user. Light edits for clarity or context from the conversation are fine. Do not substantially rewrite it.\n\n## Output Format\n\nThe explanation uses the sections defined in `references/explainer-prompt.md`, dropping any that do not apply: Overview, Key Concepts, How It Works, Where Things Live, Gotchas.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-3.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-3.txt",
      "sha256": "5a799584adc07c774c4870846e34f0a8f8c82a75d151721d1aa225bb983523d2",
      "bytes": 2793,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "# Explorer Prompt Template\n\nBuild each explorer worker's prompt from this template. Fill in the placeholders.\n\n---\n\nYou are exploring a codebase to understand how something works. Gather facts. Trace code paths, read implementations, map components. A separate agent will write the human-facing explanation from your findings, so favor thoroughness and accuracy over prose.\n\nWhen independent workers are available, other explorers investigate different slices in parallel. In reduced capability, the coordinator explores distinct slices sequentially and discloses that limit. Don't try to cover everything. Focus on your assigned angle and go deep.\n\n## Question\n\n> {QUESTION}\n\n## Your Exploration Angle\n\n{EXPLORATION_ANGLE}\n\n## Exploration Instructions\n\nStart by finding the relevant code. Use available file discovery, symbol search and file-reading tools to understand the actual implementation. Don't guess from names. Read the code.\n\nFollow this pattern:\n1. **Find the entry point.** What triggers this behavior? A user action, an API call, a scheduled job? Find where it starts.\n2. **Trace the flow.** Follow the call chain from the entry point. Read each function. Understand what data flows through and how it transforms.\n3. **Map the key abstractions.** What types, interfaces, services, or classes are central? Read their definitions. Understand what they represent and why they exist.\n4. **Find the boundaries.** Where does this subsystem interface with others? What goes in, what comes out?\n5. **Look for the non-obvious.** Anything surprising? Anything that looks like a historical artifact? Anything a newcomer would misunderstand?\n\nKeep exploring until you can describe the full picture without hand-waving. If you hit a part you can't trace, say so explicitly. \"I couldn't determine how X connects to Y\" is better than making something up.\n\n## Output\n\nReturn your findings in this structure. Be factual and specific. Reference exact file paths, function names, type names, and line numbers where relevant.\n\n### Components Found\nThe key types, services, classes, and abstractions. For each: name, file path, and a one-sentence description of what it does.\n\n### Flow\nThe execution flow step by step. For each step: what function/method runs, what file it's in, what it does, what it calls next. Include the data that flows between steps.\n\n### Files Read\nEvery file you read during exploration, so the explainer can reference them.\n\n### Boundaries\nWhere this subsystem connects to other parts of the codebase. The inputs and outputs.\n\n### Non-Obvious Things\nAnything surprising, historically motivated, or easy to get wrong. Things that look like they should work one way but work another.\n\n### Open Questions\nAnything you couldn't fully trace or understand. Be honest about gaps.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-4.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-4.txt",
      "sha256": "08618881df380b5c74a030d6a577fd07f0fc6a3a91b4d0ec1795da3c6350c9b1",
      "bytes": 3411,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "# Explainer Prompt Template\n\nBuild the explainer worker's prompt from this template. Fill in the placeholders.\n\n---\n\nYou are writing an architectural explanation for a senior engineer. For complex questions, explorers have traced different slices and gathered findings, independently when supported or sequentially with an explicit reduced-capability disclosure. For simple questions, omit Explorer Findings and explore the source yourself in this role. Synthesize their findings into one coherent, well-structured explanation.\n\n## Original Question\n\n> {QUESTION}\n\n## Explorer Findings\n\n{EXPLORER_FINDINGS_ALL}\n\n## Instructions\n\nThe explorers each investigated a different angle of the same subsystem. Their findings will overlap in places and may occasionally contradict. Reconcile them. Merge overlapping descriptions, resolve contradictions by checking the code yourself, and combine the separate slices into a unified picture.\n\nWrite an explanation a senior engineer unfamiliar with this area could read and walk away with a solid mental model, understanding the architecture well enough to start working in it confidently.\n\nYou have read-only access to the codebase to check anything, clarify a detail, or fill a gap. Use available file reading, symbol search and file discovery as needed. When explorer findings are supplied, you should not need to re-explore from scratch. Without them, trace entry points, calls, data, abstractions and boundaries yourself; disclose anything you cannot trace.\n\n## Output Format\n\nUse this structure, adapted to what makes sense for the question. Not every section is needed for every question.\n\n### Overview\n1-2 paragraphs. What is this thing, what does it do, why does it exist. Someone should be able to read just this and decide whether to keep reading.\n\n### Key Concepts\nThe important types, services, or abstractions needed to follow the rest. Brief definitions, not exhaustive.\n\n### How It Works\nThe core of the explanation, and the longest section. Walk through the flow: what triggers it, what happens step by step, where data goes, what the decision points are.\n\nUse prose, not pseudocode. Reference specific files and functions so the reader knows where to look, but don't dump large code blocks unless a snippet is essential to a point.\n\nWhen the flow involves multiple components talking to each other, or data transforming through stages, include a diagram. Use mermaid (```mermaid) for structured flows (sequence diagrams, flowcharts, component graphs) or ASCII art for simpler relationships where mermaid would be overkill. Use your judgment. A diagram should clarify, not decorate. If prose covers the flow, skip the diagram.\n\n### Where Things Live\nA brief file/directory map. Just the ones someone would need to start working here.\n\n### Gotchas\nNon-obvious things, surprising behavior, historical context, pitfalls. Skip this section if there's nothing worth calling out.\n\n## Communication Style\n\n- Use concrete language, not abstractions-about-abstractions\n- Say \"the `UserService` calls `AuthClient.refresh()`\" not \"the service delegates to the client\"\n- When something is complex, explain why it's complex. Don't just describe the complexity\n- When something is simple, don't pad it out\n- If there's a helpful analogy, use it. If there isn't, don't force one\n- If the explorers flagged open questions or gaps, acknowledge them rather than hiding them\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-5.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-5.txt",
      "sha256": "81ecaa398ff10882a1e1050c529c04c3a678eecc03e36ad24549379c3776d106",

      "text": "---\nname: benchmark-checklist\ndescription: \"Vet a perf measurement (limiter, tuning, limits, errors, repeatability, relevance, and whether the work happened) before you report or act on it. Use when you run a benchmark or report a speedup or regression you measured.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Benchmark checklist\n\nUse this when you produce a performance number: a PR's before and after, a regression claim, a hillclimb harness, or a library or config choice. [Explain the Number](../principle-explain-the-number/SKILL.md) says why. Answer each question below with evidence from a run, not from a guess about the code.\n\nFor a quick ballpark the user asked for, one run is enough. Still check questions 4 and 7, and say that it is one run. Skip the rest unless that run looks wrong. A choice between options is never a ballpark.\n\n## Capability and permission checks\n\nUse [host-capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to check available inspection, execution, profiling and evidence storage before a run. Use commands only when exposed and authorized. The commands below are examples for compatible systems; use an available platform equivalent when a command is missing. Do not install profilers or stop unrelated processes without authorization. If a check is unavailable, name it and retain the report gates below. Reviewing supplied artifacts can produce a reduced assessment, but cannot supply missing run evidence. If neither artifacts nor authorized inspection are available, report the measurement as blocked.\n\nRecord the workload, data size, concurrency, timed region, exact revisions and configurations of every side, and sample count with the run evidence. Check machine contention, tuning, caches, warmup, errors and completed work before claiming a measured improvement. Preserve `disable-model-invocation: true` as source metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\n\n## Before you run anything\n\n- Write down the claim you expect to make, in the words you would ship (\"export is 30% faster at p50 on the 60k-row dataset\"). The questions test that sentence.\n- Read the measurement script. Note what it times, what it counts, and what it ignores.\n- Check the load average with `uptime` and the core count with `nproc`. If the machine is busy, find out what is running. If you cannot stop it, interleave the sides so both see the same noise, and say so in the report.\n\n## The questions\n\n1. **Why not double?** Name the limiter. Profile in a run you do not report, because profilers and tracers slow the work down. Use CPU per process (`top`, `pidstat`), a profiler for the runtime (`node --cpu-prof`, `py-spy`, `perf`), I/O wait, and syscall counts (`strace -c` on Linux). Then map the hot spot to source. Watch the load generator too. If it saturates first, you measured the load generator. If a change did not move the number, the limiter explains why, so find it before you call the change useless.\n2. **Was it tuned?** Run every side the way production runs it: release builds, production flags and env, batching and transaction settings, connection pools, caches as warm or cold as production sees them, and the same versions and data. If one side runs on defaults, you compared configurations, not implementations. A limiter that is a setting, such as a commit per row, a debug build, or a missing index, means that side is untuned. Tune it and measure again before you pick a winner. If you cannot tune it, do not pick a winner from that run. Narrowing the claim to the code as it ships today does not fix this when the user is choosing what to adopt, because they adopt the option, not today's settings.\n3. **Did it break limits?** Do the arithmetic. Compare bytes per second with disk and network bandwidth, and operations per second times the cost per operation with the cores you have. Compare the time saved with the time the changed piece took. Removing a piece that takes 10% of the run can make the run at most about 11% faster. A result past a limit means the run measured something other than the work, such as a cache, a no-op, or a bug.\n4. **Did it error?** Count failures and non-success responses, and check that the outputs are correct, not just present. Errors behave differently from successes. Rejections are often fast, and timeouts and retries are slow. If the script does not count errors, add the count.\n5. **Does it reproduce?** Run each side at least 5 times, and alternate the sides (A, B, A, B, and so on) so that warmup, lazy initialization, caches, and drift do not favor one side. Report the median and the range. Treat a gap smaller than the run-to-run variation as no measurable difference. When the call is close, use a rank-sum test or the harness's own statistics.\n6. **Does it matter?** Next to any micro result, measure the end-to-end path a user waits on, with realistic data sizes and concurrency. Report the micro result as a share of the whole. A helper that takes 1% of a request can make the request at most 1% faster, however fast the helper gets.\n7. **Did it even happen?** Confirm the work ran inside the timed region. The request reached the server, the rows were written, the bytes were read, and the code used the result. Lazy code (generators nobody iterates, promises nobody awaits, results the JIT can discard) and timeouts all produce numbers for work that never happened.\n\n## Report\n\n- Lead with the verdict: faster, slower, no measurable difference, or inconclusive.\n- Give the number with its unit, the run count, the range, and the limiter. For example, \"p50 41 ms → 33 ms, median of 7 runs per side, range 32 to 35 ms after, bound by JSON parsing on one core.\"\n- Call the verdict inconclusive when you claim a difference but cannot name the limiter, when a side ran untuned, or when you could not check questions 4 and 7. Name the gap.\n- Keep a PR body to one primary number, per the **Opening a PR** playbook. Put the runs, the range, and the limiter evidence in a linked artifact or a notes file.\n\n## How this fits the other perf material\n\n- The **Perf issue** playbook finds and fixes slowness, and the performance mantras in its step 2 generate the fixes. This skill vets its baseline before the playbook plans from it, and every number after that.\n- The **Hillclimb** playbook loops on one metric. This skill vets its harness before the harness is frozen. The frozen harness then prints error and work counts, so each keep-or-revert checks questions 4 and 7 for free.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-6.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-6.txt",
      "sha256": "c40999b0f55ca55f74c06ddad12acfd6c022c23a26d9ac4372a3fa332987f4a5",
      "bytes": 2590,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "---\nname: principle-explain-the-number\ndescription: \"Apply before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured: a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result. Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Explain the Number\n\nA measured number is a claim about a system. Before you trust it, report it, or act on it, find what limits it and rule out that it measured something else.\n\n**Why:** A run that went wrong still prints a plausible number. Requests that failed, a cache that skipped the work, code that never ran, a side left on default settings, and run-to-run noise all produce results that look fine. If you cannot say why the number is not twice as good, you do not know what you measured.\n\n**Pattern:**\n\n- **Ask \"why not double?\"** Name the resource or code path that bounds the result, such as a core, a lock, the disk, the network, or the load generator itself. Get it from a profile or from system counters taken during a run, then map it to source. A guess from reading the code is not a limiter.\n- **List what else the number could be measuring, and rule out each one with evidence.** The usual suspects are errors, skipped or cached work, an untuned side, noise, and a piece too small to matter end to end.\n- **Keep the evidence with the number.** Put the run count, the spread, and the limiter in the notes or a linked artifact, so a reader can check the claim.\n\nFor a performance number, run the full procedure with the [benchmark-checklist](../benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md) skill. For an eval result, ask the same of the trials: did every run do the task, does the gap hold across trials and models, and does the scenario matter.\n\nYou skipped this when the evidence behind a number has no run count, no spread, or no named limiter, or when the time saved is larger than the time the changed piece took.\n\nDistinct from [Prove It Works](../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md), which checks that an output is real. This checks that a measured number means what you say it means.\n\n## Session capabilities\n\nUse [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to check available inspection, execution and evidence storage within the authorized scope. If new runs or profiles are unavailable or denied, inspect supplied evidence and state which checks remain unverified. Do not guess a limiter from source or report an unsupported speedup. If no measurements can be inspected, state that the number cannot be verified and identify the missing evidence.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-7.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-7.txt",
      "sha256": "3c4f05ffecc162b6ae376b7adcf4e7d030c8dfc65a7f26f8519f2c1544c3b98b",
      "bytes": 8456,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "---\nname: show-me-your-work\ndescription: \"Keep a reviewable decision trail for long-running or unattended work: a TSV log with one row per decision (what, why, evidence, result). Local by default; commit it when a reviewer needs the trail to trust the result. Use for /show-me-your-work, autonomous or multi-phase runs, or work a human reviews after stepping away.\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n# Show me your work\n\nKeep one canonical log.\n\n## The format\n\nA single TSV file, one row per decision. Cells stay single-line. Evidence is a pointer, not prose.\n\nCopy [references/decision-log-template.tsv](references/decision-log-template.tsv) (the header row) to start a clean log. Columns:\n\n- **ts.** ISO8601 timestamp.\n- **phase.** The phase or workstream.\n- **decision.** What was chosen or done, one line.\n- **why.** The reason in plain words. If a principle drove it, say it plainly, not as a jargon tag.\n- **evidence.** A link or path that proves it: commit SHA, PR number, `file:line`, or an artifact, trace, or screenshot path. Never a paragraph.\n- **result.** The outcome or predicate state: `tests green`, `reverted`, `pixel-diff 0`, `INCONCLUSIVE`, `open`.\n\nAn example, plain-spoken so a reviewer reads it at a glance.\n\n```\nts\tphase\tdecision\twhy\tevidence\tresult\n2026-05-24T09:02:00Z\tframe\tcounted the work first, about 100 components and roughly 75 hours\twanted to know the size before starting a long run\tcommit 3a9f1c2\tfound 5 things to sort out before starting\n2026-05-24T09:40:00Z\tharness\ttook screenshots of the old version before changing anything\tso we can compare old against new and catch any visual change\tscripts/snapshot.sh, baseline/\tsaved 120 reference screenshots\n2026-05-24T11:15:00Z\twidget\tmoved the widget styles over without changing how it looks\tkeep the change small and the result identical\tcommit 7c21e0a, pixel-diff 0\tlooks identical, tests pass\n2026-05-24T12:30:00Z\twidget\tthrew out a helper's work because its screenshots were blank\tchecked the real files instead of trusting its summary\tworktree reset\treverted, tightened the instructions for next time\n```\n\n## Logging a row\n\nWrite each entry the way you'd tell a teammate what you did. Plain words, concrete actions, no AI speak or abstract jargon (the **unslop** skill applies to log text too).\n\nUse the helper [scripts/log.sh](scripts/log.sh) with `<logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>` through an authorized execution binding. Pass an explicit nonempty local output path that this task owns. The helper rejects tabs, newlines and carriage returns in any argument before creating directories or writing bytes. It stamps `ts`, writes the header on first use, preserves previous rows, and prefixes any cell starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, or `@` with a single quote. Use this helper for generated or user-supplied cells so validation and formula protection apply consistently. Host execution still requires an authorized binding.\n\nLog decision points and checkpoints, not every action: a fork chosen, a unit completed with its verification result, a pivot or revert with its trigger, a blocker surfaced, a gate fixed. For loop runs, one row per iteration. Skip the trivial and self-evident.\n\nA run is one agent conversation, including its later turns and any summary of it. A pickup, a replacement agent, or a new chat starts a new run. When a run adds to a log that already has rows, its first row has phase `start`, and so does its first row after another run's `start` row. So a run that comes back to a log in a later turn first reads the log's last rows to see whether another run wrote since. A `start` row names the `ts` range of the rows before it that this run did not write, and its evidence names this run, such as its agent id. Use phase `start` for nothing else.\n\n## Where it lives\n\nBy default the log is a working artifact, not committed. Keep it at `decisions.tsv` in the work dir, or `.audit/<task-slug>.tsv` when several efforts run at once, and leave it out of git.\n\nCommit it only when the work is ambitious enough that a reviewer needs the trail to trust the result.\n\n## Rules\n\n- Append-only. A wrong call gets a new row that supersedes it. Never edit or delete history.\n- Prefer evidence produced by committed scripts over hand-made one-offs (the **encode-lessons-in-structure** principle skill).\n\n## Audit the log against the transcript\n\nAt the end of the run, before handing back, check the log told the truth. Discover this run's transcript through the current session's history binding, or an explicitly identified transcript in the active workspace. The source used `agent-transcripts/`; do not assume the host provides that directory. Never search unrelated private chats or global transcript directories. If this run's transcript is inaccessible or incomplete, record the gap and do not claim a complete transcript audit. Walk this run's rows against what actually happened. Each stretch of them begins at one of this run's `start` rows, or at the first row if this run created the log, and ends at the next `start` row of another run:\n\n- Check that every row maps to a real decision or action.\n- Check that each row's evidence resolves and shows what the row claims.\n- A fork, pivot, or abandoned approach that shaped the work but isn't logged is a gap. Add it.\n\nCorrect the log, not the story. The audit never edits or removes a row, even an invented one. When a row records neither a real decision nor a real action, or its claim or evidence is wrong, add a row that supersedes it with what actually happened and a pointer that resolves. This audit does not check rows outside this run's stretches. If this run's own work shows one of them is wrong, supersede it like any wrong call.\n\n## Cross-model review of the trail\n\nBefore handing back, use an authorized exposed independent-worker binding to request a reviewer on a different model family from the one that did the work. Follow [model selection](../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Observe the worker identity, result and actual model family before claiming independent cross-model review. If no authorized worker or verified different family is available, record that review as blocked. An authorized sequential scan is reduced capability and does not satisfy this review requirement. Self-review is not a substitute. The subagent reads the audit trail and the run's transcript, then flags what the user should pay attention to. Not a redo of the work, a scan for what's suboptimal or risky.\n\n- Decisions logged with weak or absent evidence.\n- Verification steps skipped or claimed without proof in the transcript.\n- Choices that look risky in hindsight (premature, scope-creeping, papering over a symptom).\n- Gaps the user would otherwise miss on a casual skim.\n\nEvery reply for a run that produced a trail ends with an \"Attention\" section. Lead with the reviewer's model on its own line (`reviewed by <model>`), then list each flag pointing to specific rows or moments. \"No flags\" is a valid value. The model name is not.\n\n## Reviewing the trail\n\nRead top to bottom, follow the evidence pointers, spot-check. GitHub renders a committed TSV as a table. `column -s$'\\t' -t decisions.tsv` renders it in a terminal.\n\n## Composing this skill\n\nOther skills route their audit trail here instead of inventing one. Reference it by name and let it own the format. Don't restate the columns.\n\n## Host capabilities and safety\n\nUse [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for history, execution, persistence and reviewer bindings. Write only to an authorized local path, then read back the saved trail before claiming persistence. A log is evidence of recorded decisions, not proof of unattended execution or recovery. Do not include credentials, tokens, raw secret-bearing output or unrelated private data. Use a safe evidence pointer or a redacted local artifact instead. Record only observed results; unresolved predicates stay `open` or `INCONCLUSIVE`.\n\nThe default local trail does not authorize a commit. Commit, publish, message or other external action requires explicit authorization. Preserve append-only history and scope every transcript audit to this run. When review is blocked, keep the Attention section and state `reviewed by unknown` if no actual reviewer model was observed, followed by the capability gap. Never use `No flags` to imply an unperformed review.\n"
    },

      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-8.txt",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/read-8.txt",
      "sha256": "fb47611cbb75ea17aa80cb4f8dbfc7978b8844450b0ce702961bdef65cbb1342",
      "bytes": 1717,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Append a well-formed row to a show-me-your-work decision log (TSV).\n# Usage: log.sh <logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>\nset -euo pipefail\n\nif [ \"$#\" -ne 6 ]; then\n\tprintf 'usage: log.sh <logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>\\n' >&2\n\texit 1\nfi\n\n# The caller owns the output path. Validate every argument before any write.\nif [ -z \"$1\" ]; then\n    printf 'error: an explicit owned output path is required\\n' >&2\n    exit 1\nfi\nfor value in \"$@\"; do\n    case \"$value\" in\n        *$'\\t'*|*$'\\n'*|*$'\\r'*)\n            printf 'error: tabs, newlines and carriage returns are not allowed\\n' >&2\n            exit 1\n            ;;\n    esac\ndone\nlogfile=\"$1\"\nshift\n\nlogdir=\"$(dirname \"$logfile\")\"\nif [ -n \"$logdir\" ] && [ \"$logdir\" != \".\" ] && [ ! -d \"$logdir\" ]; then\n\tmkdir -p \"$logdir\"\nfi\n\n# Use `>>` here, never `>`. A network mount can fail this test for a log\n# that exists. Then the cost is one stray header line, not the rows.\nif [ ! -s \"$logfile\" ]; then\n\tprintf 'ts\\tphase\\tdecision\\twhy\\tevidence\\tresult\\n' >> \"$logfile\"\nfi\n\nts=\"$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)\"\n# Record separators were rejected above. Prefix any cell\n# whose first char a spreadsheet would parse as a formula (=, +, -, @)\n# with a single quote. The skill expects this log to be read in\n# spreadsheets, so attacker-controlled evidence (PR titles, filenames,\n# generated text) must not become formula execution when a reviewer\n# opens the file.\nclean() {\n\tlocal v\n\tv=\"$1\"\n\tcase \"$v\" in\n\t\t=*|+*|-*|@*) printf \"'%s\" \"$v\" ;;\n\t\t*) printf '%s' \"$v\" ;;\n\tesac\n}\nprintf '%s\\t%s\\t%s\\t%s\\t%s\\t%s\\n' \\\n\t\"$ts\" \"$(clean \"$1\")\" \"$(clean \"$2\")\" \"$(clean \"$3\")\" \"$(clean \"$4\")\" \"$(clean \"$5\")\" \\\n\t>> \"$logfile\"\n"
    },

      "text": "The prepared 60000-row export measured 17.305458 ms before and 10.618542 ms after in the final five alternating runs per side. The calculated reduction is 38.640503 percent. Ranges were 16.857833 to 18.723375 ms before and 10.153958 to 11.010625 ms after. All final samples produced the exact 1168911 CSV bytes, with 60000 completed rows and zero reported errors.\n\nThe fixed target is at least 50 percent lower latency, with at least 10 attempts. It remains unmet. Eleven measured trials produced two retained local stages and nine rejected candidates. One retained stage came from a fresh independent native worker. Later direct trials exercised the restricted-worker variant and do not satisfy independent delegation. The first two valid gains were below target, so no valid lucky early target win occurred. The tempting skipped-work result was rejected.\n\nThe retained stages are numeric-field specialization and a per-export customer-escape cache. The current artifact is export-active.mjs. Source inputs remain at ba7d1f5b02349fbac46954023c1ac94bd677aba2, and all prepared candidates have recorded content hashes. No new treatment Git revision exists because commits are forbidden.\n\nThe loop stopped for marginal cost after the listed cheap encoder variants failed to improve the retained cache. The final profile placed 38 of 72 samples in JSON.parse. The next idea is streaming parsing or partitioned encoding, which requires a new architecture exploration and fresh validation. This stop does not meet or relax the target. The unique-customer workload is materially different and produced only a limited raw comparison. It cannot replace the fixed-workload result.\n\nThe canonical append-only trail is /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/decision.tsv. The real log helper ran and saved rows with readback. In the missing-persistence branch it exited 1 and saved no log. The denied-execution branch ran no benchmark. Wrong CSV and skipped-work candidates were rejected. No commits, PR writes or schedules occurred. local-pr-draft.md is a local draft only.\n\nBuild the Lever produced prepare.mjs, measure.mjs and gate.mjs. Explain the Number kept profiler runs separate from timing samples and rejected skipped work and noisy results. Sequence Verifiable Units kept each measured trial separate. Separate Before Serializing Shared State gave the worker candidate01 ownership and retained a single log writer. Laziness Protocol rejected a nested suffix cache that did not advance the result.\n\n## Attention\n\nreviewed by unknown\n\nDifferent-family independent trail review is blocked because actual worker model families were not observed. The current native transcript snapshot retains actual tool calls and outputs, but host-encrypted message fields prevent a complete readable transcript audit. Full supplied dispatch text is retained separately. Matching control-cli and create-skill companions are unavailable. Independent how exploration was reduced to a sequential read-only explanation. The newly prepared multi-file harness did not receive prior architect exploration. Its tests and local measurements do not close that workflow gate. Only candidate01 had an independent owner; subsequent local trials remain reduced capability. The compiler ran a real noEmit check and exited 0, but it does not prove CSV behavior or performance.\n"

      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/supplied-input.json",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/supplied-input.json",

      ],
      "text": "{\n  \"contract\": {\n    \"id\": \"bounded-improvement\",\n    \"sourcePaths\": [\n      \"skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/hillclimb.md\",\n      \"skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md\",\n      \"skills/how/SKILL.md\",\n      \"skills/how/references/explorer-prompt.md\",\n      \"skills/how/references/explainer-prompt.md\",\n      \"skills/benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md\",\n      \"skills/principle-explain-the-number/SKILL.md\",\n      \"skills/show-me-your-work/SKILL.md\",\n      \"skills/show-me-your-work/scripts/log.sh\",\n      \"skills/show-me-your-work/references/decision-log-template.tsv\",\n      \"skills/principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md\",\n      \"skills/principle-build-the-lever/SKILL.md\",\n      \"skills/principle-guard-the-context-window/SKILL.md\",\n      \"skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md\",\n      \"skills/principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md\",\n      \"skills/principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md\",\n      \"skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/perf-issue.md\",\n      \"skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/autonomous-run.md\",\n      \"skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/opening-a-pr.md\",\n      \"agents/poteto-agent.md\"\n    ],\n    \"prompt\": \"Hillclimb a fixture export latency using a fixed target and minimum iteration count. Supply contrasting realistic workloads, an exact baseline revision, a repeatable harness, error and completed-work counts, a green regression gate and candidate artifacts. Include an early lucky win, noise-sized improvement, changed workload, regression, plateau, worthwhile cheap remaining hypothesis and exhausted marginal ideas. Permit local measurement and edits but no commits, PR writes or recurring scheduling. Repeat with unavailable workers, denied execution and missing persistence. Compare every candidate using the same valid frozen metric and retain the stopping rule.\",\n    \"requirements\": [\n      \"Read the complete mode, playbook, how prompts, measurement and principle leaves, poteto role prompt and decision-log helper. Missing required reads stop dependent work.\",\n      \"Reproduce the complaint across realistic workload dimensions. Agree one metric, direction, target and minimum attempt floor before attempts. Prove harness sensitivity with contrasting workloads, vet the checklist, freeze command/configuration, record baseline and green gate.\",\n      \"Use exact revisions and comparable workloads. For performance comparisons alternate at least five runs per side, retain median/range, limiter, production tuning, outputs, error and completed-work counts. Source inspection and inconclusive comparisons cannot prove gains.\",\n      \"Open gitignored decision.tsv through show-me-your-work and log.sh. Preserve append-only history, per-attempt id/hypothesis/change/before/after/delta/tests/verdict/note, read before attempts, readback persistence and transcript/review limits.\",\n      \"Ground hypotheses in how. For performance use Perf issue mantra order without its earlier-success stop rule. Delegate tightly scoped revision-matched attempts with full role prompt, supported configured or inherited selection and inspected results. Isolate concurrent hypotheses and compare each to the same base; sequential fallback stays reduced.\",\n      \"Measure and gate each single change before the next. Keep only changes past noise with green tests, revert failed candidates fully without touching unrelated work, and log every verdict. Read frozen source contracts, verify committed input, prepare and inspect sensitive benchmark, profile outside report timings, freeze target and attempt floor, establish green literal CSV gate.\n- Independent workstreams. Read-only how explanation and single-function candidate use distinct owned artifacts after a worker slot is available. Serial measured attempts prevent contention and unverified stacking.\n- Shared mutable state. Only this case owner writes the canonical decision.tsv, measurement outputs and integrated export-active.mjs. Workers own isolated directories.\n- Smallest safe decomposition. One candidate owner changes one exportCsv body. No design boundary change is needed for the first hypothesis. One coordinator measures and inspects each candidate before any next attempt.\n\nThe package's matching control-cli companion and create-skill companion are not available in this session. Local Bun execution and native profiler are observed controls, with a reduced matching companion gate. No external delivery is authorized. Requested worker selection inherits the parent. Actual model and effort remain unknown unless tool output exposes them.\n"

      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/trial10.mjs",

      "owners": [

      ],
      "text": "export function exportCsv(orders) {\n  const suffixes=new Map();\n  return 'id,customer,total\\n'+orders.map(o=>{\n    let totals=suffixes.get(o.customer);\n    if(!totals){totals=new Map();suffixes.set(o.customer,totals)}\n    let suffix=totals.get(o.total);\n    if(suffix===undefined){\n      const customer=/[\",\\r\\n]/.test(o.customer)?'\"'+o.customer.replaceAll('\"','\"\"')+'\"':o.customer;\n      suffix=','+customer+','+o.total;\n      totals.set(o.total,suffix);\n    }\n    return o.id+suffix;\n  }).join('\\n');\n}\n"
    },

'\"' + o.customer.replaceAll('\"','\"\"') + '\"' : o.customer;\n      customers[o.customer]=customer;\n    }\n    return o.id + ',' + customer + ',' + o.total;\n  }).join('\\n');\n}\n"

      "text": "# Restricted variants\n\nExecution denied variant. In this separately scoped branch, no benchmark execution is authorized. The response is that a baseline and treatment cannot be newly measured under the denial. Existing baseline.json supports only its recorded local workload. No unrun candidate has a new timing or green gate. No command denial is fabricated, and no execution is attempted inside this branch.\n\nWorkers unavailable variant. Native workers are available in the actual host and the first candidate is independently delegated. The separately scoped variant withholds that binding. Later direct candidate trials can exercise the same full reads, frozen harness, gate and log sequentially. Their measured artifacts cannot establish independent delegation or unknown model diversity. This is an authorization/capability reduction scenario, rather than a claim that actual native dispatch failed.\n\nPersistence missing variant. The real log helper is run against an owned path whose parent is an inert regular file. It exits 1 with a mkdir failure. No trail was saved at that path. missing-persistence.json retains stderr, absent-file result and verification that the canonical trail is unchanged. Required saved-decision continuity is blocked in that branch. No candidate can be accepted from an unsaved log.\n"
    },

      "bytes": 632,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "import { resolve } from 'node:path';\nconst [moduleFile, dataFile, outputFile] = process.argv.slice(2);\nconst { exportCsv } = await import(resolve(moduleFile));\nconst start = performance.now();\nconst orders = JSON.parse(await Bun.file(dataFile).text());\nconst csv = exportCsv(orders);\nawait Bun.write(outputFile, csv);\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify({ milliseconds: performance.now() - start, completedRows: orders.length, errors: 0, bytes: Buffer.byteLength(csv), inputRevision: 'ba7d1f5b02349fbac46954023c1ac94bd677aba2', sourceSha256: new Bun.CryptoHasher('sha256').update(await Bun.file(moduleFile).arrayBuffer()).digest('hex') }));\n"

      "text": "{\n  \"inputRevision\": \"ba7d1f5b02349fbac46954023c1ac94bd677aba2\",\n  \"metric\": \"median end-to-end export milliseconds\",\n  \"direction\": \"lower\",\n  \"targetPercentReduction\": 50,\n  \"minimumAttempts\": 10,\n  \"workloadRows\": 60000,\n  \"concurrency\": 1,\n  \"environment\": {\n    \"NODE_ENV\": \"production\"\n  },\n  \"warmup\": 1,\n  \"samplesPerSide\": 5,\n  \"cache\": \"warm filesystem, fresh Bun process per sample\",\n  \"timedRegion\": \"input file read and JSON parse through awaited CSV write; excludes process startup/module load\",\n  \"claim\": \"60k-row local CSV export latency is at least 50% lower with correct CSV bytes\",\n  \"stop\": \"target reduction >=50% AND measured attempts >=10, otherwise explicit blocker or marginal-cost stop\",\n  \"gitRevisionPolicy\": \"no new commits; input SHA plus immutable content SHA256 identifies prepared baseline/treatments\"\n}"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/variant-unique/measure.mjs",
      "originalExternalPath": "/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-evaluation-20261016/fixtures/durable-04/bounded-improvement/variant-unique/measure.mjs",
      "sha256": "ced7169e2a492b4bc96fc345941929c7c18b8688f6e2b85c6d5c322eb5da6d06",
      "bytes": 2178,
      "owners": [
        "grades-native-durable-04.json#1"
      ],
      "text": "import { resolve } from 'node:path';\nconst dir=import.meta.dir;\nconst [candidate='export-base.mjs',name='baseline',rows='60000']=process.argv.slice(2);\nconst result={name,inputRevision:'ba7d1f5b02349fbac46954023c1ac94bd677aba2',configuration:await Bun.file(dir+'/freeze.json').json(),rows:Number(rows),baseline:[],candidate:[],checks:[]};\nconst sha=async p=>new Bun.CryptoHasher('sha256').update(await Bun.file(p).arrayBuffer()).digest('hex');\nresult.workloadSha256=await sha(dir+'/orders-'+rows+'.json');\nresult.baselineSourceSha256=await sha(dir+'/export-base.mjs');result.candidateSourceSha256=await sha(resolve(dir,candidate));\nfor(let i=-1;i<5;i++) for(const side of ['baseline','candidate']){\n const file=side==='baseline'?'export-base.mjs':candidate;\n const output=dir+'/output-'+name+'-'+side+'.csv';\n const p=Bun.spawnSync(['bun','--no-install',dir+'/export-cli.mjs',resolve(dir,file),dir+'/orders-'+rows+'.json',output],{env:{...process.env,NODE_ENV:'production'}});\n let sample; try {sample=JSON.parse(p.stdout.toString())}catch{sample={error:p.stderr.toString()}};\n const correct=p.exitCode===0 && Buffer.compare(Buffer.from(await Bun.file(output).arrayBuffer()),Buffer.from(await Bun.file(dir+'/expected-'+rows+'.csv').arrayBuffer()))===0 && sample.completedRows===Number(rows) && sample.errors===0;\n result.checks.push({side,index:i,exitCode:p.exitCode,stdout:p.stdout.toString(),stderr:p.stderr.toString(),correct});\n if(i>=0)result[side].push(sample);\n}\nconst stats=a=>{const times=a.map(x=>x.milliseconds).sort((a,b)=>a-b);return {median:times[2],min:times[0],max:times[4],range:times[4]-times[0]}};\nresult.stats={baseline:stats(result.baseline),candidate:stats(result.candidate)};\nconst a=result.stats.baseline,b=result.stats.candidate;\nresult.deltaPercent=100*(a.median-b.median)/a.median;\nresult.gapBeyondRange=Math.abs(a.median-b.median)>Math.max(a.range,b.range);\nresult.green=result.checks.every(x=>x.correct);\nawait Bun.write(dir+'/'+name+'.json',JSON.stringify(result,null,2));\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify({name,stats:result.stats,deltaPercent:result.deltaPercent,gapBeyondRange:result.gapBeyondRange,green:result.green,artifact:dir+'/'+name+'.json'}));\n"
    },

      "text": "const d=import.meta.dir;\nconst supplied=await Bun.file(d+'/supplied-input.json').json();\nconst base='/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-f462363/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.0/';\nconst records=[];\nfor(const path of supplied.sourcePaths){\n const corrected=path.replace('agents/poteto-agent.md','references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md');\n const bytes=await Bun.file(base+corrected).arrayBuffer();\n const p=Bun.spawnSync(['git','-C','/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-openai','show','f4623636a287bea2aa79c4b2234a53d20a5f193c:'+corrected]);\n records.push({path:base+corrected,freeze:'f4623636a287bea2aa79c4b2234a53d20a5f193c',exitCode:p.exitCode,identical:p.exitCode===0&&Buffer.compare(Buffer.from(bytes),p.stdout)===0,sha256:new Bun.CryptoHasher('sha256').update(bytes).digest('hex')});\n}\nawait Bun.write(d+'/source-verification.json',JSON.stringify(records,null,2));\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(records));\nif(records.some(x=>!x.identical))process.exit(1);\n"
